* 1 ≤ | S | ≤ 10 ^ 6. However, | S | represents the length of the character string S.
* S consists only of uppercase or lowercase alphabets.



Since the input may be very large, it is recommended to use a high-speed function to receive the input.

Output format

If S is a character string, output "Love AB!" For A and B that satisfy S = ABABA. However, if multiple pairs of A and B satisfy the condition, output the one with the smallest | AB |. If S is not a Mikomi string, output "mitomerarenaiWA".

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We need to determine if a string S can be written in the form ABABA where A and B are non-empty strings
2. If it can be written in this form, we output "Love AB!" with the smallest |AB| (length of A+B)
3. If it cannot be written in this form, we output "mitomerarenaiWA"

## Key Insights

1. The string S = ABABA means it has the pattern: A + B + A + B + A
2. The total length of S must be 5 * |AB| (since AB appears 3 times, and each AB contributes |A| + |B| to the length)
3. So |S| must be divisible by 5, and |AB| = |S| / 5
4. We need to check if we can split the string into A and B such that the pattern holds

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. **Check divisibility**: If |S| is not divisible by 5, it's impossible
2. **Calculate AB length**: |AB| = |S| / 5
3. **Extract A and B**: 
   - A should be the first |A| characters of S
   - B should be the first |B| characters of S
4. **Verify pattern**: Check if S[0:|A|] == S[|A|:|A|+|B|] == S[2*|A|:2*|A|+|B|] == S[3*|A|:3*|A|+|B|] == S[4*|A|:4*|A|+|B|]
5. **Return result**: If valid, output "Love AB!", otherwise "mitomerarenaiWA"

Wait, let me reconsider. In ABABA, the pattern is:
- Position 0 to |A|-1: A
- Position |A| to |A|+|B|-1: B  
- Position 2*|A| to 2*|A|+|B|-1: A
- Position 3*|A| to 3*|A|+|B|-1: B
- Position 4*|A| to 4*|A|+|B|-1: A

So we need to check:
- S[0:|A|] == S[2*|A|:3*|A|] == S[4*|A|:5*|A|]
- S[|A|:|A|+|B|] == S[3*|A|:4*|A|]

Actually, since we want the smallest |AB|, and |AB| = |A| + |B|, we have |S| = 5 * (|A| + |B|), so |A| + |B| = |S| / 5.

The key insight is that for a given length |AB| = k, we need:
- |A| + |B| = k
- |S| = 5k

For each possible valid k, we need to find A and B such that the pattern holds.

But since we want the smallest |AB|, and the string length determines k, we only have one possible value: k = |S| / 5.

So the algorithm is:
1. If |S| is not divisible by 5, return "mitomerarenaiWA"
2. Let k = |S| / 5 (length of AB)
3. Let |A| = i, |B| = k - i for some i in [1, k-1] (since both A and B are non-empty)
4. For each valid i, check if the pattern ABABA holds with A of length i and B of length k-i
5. Return the first (smallest |AB|) valid pair, but since |AB| is fixed, we just need to check if any valid A,B exists

Actually, since we want the smallest |AB| and |AB| is determined by |S|/5, we only need to check if there exists at least one valid partition.

## Implementation

```python
s = input().strip()
n = len(s)

# Check if length is divisible by 5
if n % 5 != 0:
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
else:
    k = n // 5  # length of AB
    
    # Try each possible length of A (from 1 to k-1)
    # Since A and B are both non-empty, |A| must be in [1, k-1]
    found = False
    result_a = ""
    result_b = ""
    
    for i in range(1, k):
        a_len = i
        b_len = k - i
        
        a = s[:a_len]
        b = s[a_len:a_len + b_len]
        
        # Check if pattern ABABA holds
        # Position 0 to |A|-1: A
        # Position |A| to |A|+|B|-1: B
        # Position 2*|A| to 2*|A|+|B|-1: A
        # Position 3*|A| to 3*|A|+|B|-1: B
        # Position 4*|A| to 4*|A|+|B|-1: A
        
        if (s[:a_len] == s[a_len:a_len+b_len] and
            s[a_len:a_len+b_len] == s[2*a_len:2*a_len+b_len] and
            s[2*a_len:2*a_len+b_len] == s[3*a_len:3*a_len+b_len] and
            s[3*a_len:3*a_len+b_len] == s[4*a_len:4*a_len+b_len]):
            
            # Actually we only need to check the pattern
            # The pattern is that positions 0,2,4*|A| should contain A
            # And positions 1,3*|A| should contain B (but we already have that)
            
            # Let me recheck: for ABABA, we have:
            # A at indices 0 to |A|-1
            # B at indices |A| to |A|+|B|-1  
            # A at indices 2*|A| to 2*|A|+|B|-1
            # B at indices 3*|A| to 3*|A|+|B|-1
            # A at indices 4*|A| to 4*|A|+|B|-1
            
            # So we need to check:
            # s[0:a_len] == s[2*a_len:2*a_len+b_len] == s[4*a_len:4*a_len+b_len]
            # s[a_len:a_len+b_len] == s[3*a_len:3*a_len+b_len]
            
            if (s[:a_len] == s[2*a_len:2*a_len+b_len] == s[4*a_len:4*a_len+b_len] and
                s[a_len:a_len+b_len] == s[3*a_len:3*a_len+b_len]):
                
                found = True
                result_a = a
                result_b = b
                break
    
    if found:
        print(f"Love {result_a}!")
    else:
        print("mitomerarenaiWA")
```

Wait, I think I'm overcomplicating this. Let me simplify:

```python
s = input().strip()
n = len(s)

# Check if length is divisible by 5
if n % 5 != 0:
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
else:
    k = n // 5  # length of AB
    
    # Try each possible length of A (from 1 to k-1)
    for i in range(1, k):
        a_len = i
        b_len = k - i
        
        a = s[:a_len]
        b = s[a_len:a_len + b_len]
        
        # Check if pattern ABABA holds
        # A at positions 0 to a_len-1
        # B at positions a_len to a_len+b_len-1  
        # A at positions 2*a_len to 2*a_len+b_len-1
        # B at positions 3*a_len to 3*a_len+b_len-1
        # A at positions 4*a_len to 4*a_len+b_len-1
        
        # Check if first A matches the third A
        if s[:a_len] != s[2*a_len:2*a_len+b_len]:
            continue
            
        # Check if first B matches the fourth B
        if s[a_len:a_len+b_len] != s[3*a_len:3*a_len+b_len]:
            continue
            
        # Check if the middle A matches the last A
        if s[2*a_len:2*a_len+b_len] != s[4*a_len:4*a_len+b_len]:
            continue
            
        # All checks passed
        print(f"Love {a}!")
        exit()
    
    # No valid A,B found
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
```

Actually, I realized the pattern: for ABABA, we have:
- A at positions 0 to |A|-1
- B at positions |A| to |A|+|B|-1
- A at positions 2*|A| to 2*|A|+|B|-1  
- B at positions 3*|A| to 3*|A|+|B|-1
- A at positions 4*|A| to 4*|A|+|B|-1

So we need:
1. s[0:|A|] == s[2*|A|:2*|A|+|B|]
2. s[|A|:|A|+|B|] == s[3*|A|:3*|A|+|B|]
3. s[2*|A|:2*|A|+|B|] == s[4*|A|:4*|A|+|B|]

Actually, since the middle A should be equal to the first A and the last A, and the middle B should be equal to the fourth B.

```python
s = input().strip()
n = len(s)

if n % 5 != 0:
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
else:
    k = n // 5  # length of AB
    
    # Try each possible length of A from 1 to k-1
    for i in range(1, k):
        a_len = i
        b_len = k - i
        
        # Extract the parts
        a = s[:a_len]
        b = s[a_len:a_len + b_len]
        
        # Check if ABABA pattern holds
        # A at positions 0, 2*a_len, 4*a_len
        # B at positions a_len, 3*a_len 
        
        if (s[:a_len] == s[2*a_len:2*a_len+b_len] == s[4*a_len:4*a_len+b_len] and
            s[a_len:a_len+b_len] == s[3*a_len:3*a_len+b_len]):
            
            print(f"Love {a}!")
            exit()
    
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
```

Submitted Code

View Extracted Code 
s = input().strip()
n = len(s)

if n % 5 != 0:
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")
else:
    k = n // 5  # length of AB
    
    # Try each possible length of A from 1 to k-1
    for i in range(1, k):
        a_len = i
        b_len = k - i
        
        # Extract the parts
        a = s[:a_len]
        b = s[a_len:a_len + b_len]
        
        # Check if ABABA pattern holds
        # A at positions 0, 2*a_len, 4*a_len
        # B at positions a_len, 3*a_len 
        
        if (s[:a_len] == s[2*a_len:2*a_len+b_len] == s[4*a_len:4*a_len+b_len] and
            s[a_len:a_len+b_len] == s[3*a_len:3*a_len+b_len]):
            
            print(f"Love {a}!")
            exit()
    
    print("mitomerarenaiWA")

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1): 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2): 4000 yen
   - B seat (type 3): 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

2. We're given 4 lines of input, each containing a ticket type (1-4) and the number of tickets sold for that type.

3. The ticket types are not necessarily given in order (S, A, B, C), so we need to map each type to its price.

4. For each line, we need to calculate the sales amount by multiplying the number of tickets by the price of that ticket type.

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. **Map ticket types to prices**: Create a dictionary to store the price for each ticket type
   - Type 1 (S) → 6000 yen
   - Type 2 (A) → 4000 yen  
   - Type 3 (B) → 3000 yen
   - Type 4 (C) → 2000 yen

2. **Process each line**: For each input line containing (type, count):
   - Look up the price for that ticket type
   - Calculate sales amount = price × count
   - Print the result

3. **Output format**: Print each sales amount on a separate line

## Implementation

```python
# Create a mapping from ticket type to price
ticket_prices = {
    1: 6000,  # S seat
    2: 4000,  # A seat
    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Read 4 lines of input and process each
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    number_of_tickets = int(line[1])
    
    # Calculate sales amount
    sales_amount = ticket_prices[ticket_type] * number_of_tickets
    
    # Output the result
    print(sales_amount)
```

## Verification with Example

Input:
- 3 10 → B seat (3000 yen) × 10 = 30,000 yen ✓
- 1 4 → S seat (6000 yen) × 4 = 24,000 yen ✓
- 4 1 → C seat (2000 yen) × 1 = 2,000 yen ✓
- 2 5 → A seat (4000 yen) × 5 = 20,000 yen ✓

Output matches exactly with the expected result.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1) = 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2) = 4000 yen  
   - B seat (type 3) = 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4) = 2000 yen

2. We're given 4 lines of input, each containing:
   - Ticket type (1-4) 
   - Number of tickets sold for that type

3. The ticket types don't appear in order (1,2,3,4), so we need to map each type to its price.

4. For each line, we need to calculate the sales amount = (price of ticket type) × (number of tickets)

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Define the price mapping
Create a dictionary to map each ticket type to its price:
- Type 1 → 6000 yen
- Type 2 → 4000 yen  
- Type 3 → 3000 yen
- Type 4 → 2000 yen

### Step 2: Process each input line
For each line:
- Read the ticket type and number
- Look up the price for that type
- Calculate sales amount = price × quantity
- Output the result

### Step 3: Implementation

```python
# Read input and calculate sales amount for each line
price_mapping = {
    1: 6000,  # S seat
    2: 4000,  # A seat
    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Process each of the 4 lines
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    quantity = int(line[1])
    
    price = price_mapping[ticket_type]
    sales_amount = price * quantity
    
    print(sales_amount)
```

- 4 1 → Type 4 (C seat) × 1 = 2000 × 1 = 2000 ✓
- 2 5 → Type 2 (A seat) × 5 = 4000 × 5 = 20000 ✓

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1): 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2): 4000 yen
   - B seat (type 3): 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

2. We receive 4 lines of input, each containing a ticket type (1-4) and the number of tickets sold.

3. The ticket types may not be in order (1, 2, 3, 4), so we need to map each type to its corresponding price.

4. For each row, we need to calculate the sales amount = (number of tickets) × (price per ticket)

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Define the price mapping**: Create a dictionary or array that maps ticket type to price
   - Type 1 → 6000 yen
   - Type 2 → 4000 yen
   - Type 3 → 3000 yen
   - Type 4 → 2000 yen

2. **Read the input**: Read 4 lines, each containing type and quantity

3. **Calculate sales amount**: For each line, multiply the type's price by the quantity

4. **Output the result**: Print each sales amount on a separate line

## Verification with Example

Input:
- 3 10 → B seat (type 3) × 10 = 3000 × 10 = 30,000 ✓
- 1 4 → S seat (type 1) × 4 = 6000 × 4 = 24,000 ✓
- 4 1 → C seat (type 4) × 1 = 2000 × 1 = 2,000 ✓
- 2 5 → A seat (type 2) × 5 = 4000 × 5 = 20,000 ✓

Output matches expected result.

```python
# Define price for each ticket type
price = {
    1: 6000,  # S seat
    2: 4000,  # A seat
    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Read 4 lines of input
for _ in range(4):
    t, n = map(int, input().split())
    sales_amount = price[t] * n
    print(sales_amount)
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1): 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2): 4000 yen
   - B seat (type 3): 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

2. We're given 4 lines of input, each containing:
   - Ticket type (1-4) and number of tickets sold

3. The types don't appear in order (1,2,3,4), so we need to map each type to its price

4. For each line, we need to calculate the sales amount = (ticket price) × (number of tickets)

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Define the price mapping**: Create a dictionary that maps ticket types to their prices
   - Type 1 → 6000 yen
   - Type 2 → 4000 yen
   - Type 3 → 3000 yen
   - Type 4 → 2000 yen

2. **Read the input**: Read 4 lines, each containing type and count

3. **Calculate sales amount**: For each line, multiply the type's price by the number of tickets

4. **Output the result**: Print each sales amount on a separate line

## Verification with Example

Input:
- 3 10 → Type 3 (B seat) × 10 = 3000 × 10 = 30,000 ✓
- 1 4 → Type 1 (S seat) × 4 = 6000 × 4 = 24,000 ✓
- 4 1 → Type 4 (C seat) × 1 = 2000 × 1 = 2,000 ✓
- 2 5 → Type 2 (A seat) × 5 = 4000 × 5 = 20,000 ✓

This matches the expected output.

## Code Implementation

```python
# Define price mapping for each ticket type
price_map = {
    1: 6000,  # S seat
    2: 4000,  # A seat
    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Read 4 lines of input
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    number_of_tickets = int(line[1])
    
    # Calculate sales amount
    sales_amount = price_map[ticket_type] * number_of_tickets
    
    # Output the result
    print(sales_amount)
```

Today is the ticket release date for Aizu Entertainment's recommended idol group "Akabeko & Koboushi". There are four types of tickets:

t1 n1
t2 n2
t3 n3
t4 n4

The integers 1, 2, 3, and 4 representing the ticket types represent S seats, A seats, B seats, and C seats, respectively. Numbers from 1 to 4 always appear once as values ​​for t1, t2, t3, and t4, but they are not always given in the order of 1, 2, 3, 4.

output

Input

3 10
1 4
4 1
2 5

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1): 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2): 4000 yen
   - B seat (type 3): 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

2. We're given 4 lines of input, each containing a ticket type (1-4) and the number of tickets sold for that type.

3. The ticket types are not guaranteed to be in order (1, 2, 3, 4), so we need to map each type to its corresponding price.

4. For each line, we need to calculate the total sales amount by multiplying the number of tickets by the price of that ticket type.

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Map ticket types to prices
- Type 1 (S seat) → 6000 yen
- Type 2 (A seat) → 4000 yen  
- Type 3 (B seat) → 3000 yen
- Type 4 (C seat) → 2000 yen

### Step 2: Process each input line
For each line with format "t n":
- Extract the ticket type (t) and number of tickets (n)
- Look up the price for ticket type t
- Calculate sales amount = n × price
- Output the result

### Step 3: Implementation

```python
# Read 4 lines of input
ticket_prices = {
    1: 6000,  # S seat
    2: 4000,  # A seat
    3: 3000,  # B seat
    4: 2000   # C seat
}

# Process each line
for _ in range(4):
    line = input().split()
    ticket_type = int(line[0])
    quantity = int(line[1])
    
    # Calculate sales amount
    sales_amount = quantity * ticket_prices[ticket_type]
    print(sales_amount)
```

## Verification with Example

Input:
- "3 10" → B seat (3000 yen) × 10 = 30,000 yen ✓
- "1 4" → S seat (6000 yen) × 4 = 24,000 yen ✓
- "4 1" → C seat (2000 yen) × 1 = 2,000 yen ✓
- "2 5" → A seat (4000 yen) × 5 = 20,000 yen ✓

Output matches expected: 30000, 24000, 2000, 20000

The solution correctly handles the unordered input and calculates the sales amount for each row.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have 4 types of tickets with different prices:
   - S seat (type 1): 6000 yen
   - A seat (type 2): 4000 yen
   - B seat (type 3): 3000 yen
   - C seat (type 4): 2000 yen

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Define the price mapping**: Create a dictionary to map ticket type numbers to their prices
   - Type 1 → 6000 yen
   - Type 2 → 4000 yen
   - Type 3 → 3000 yen
   - Type 4 → 2000 yen

2. **Read input**: Read 4 lines, each containing a type and quantity

3. **Calculate sales amount**: For each line, multiply the ticket price by the number of tickets

4. **Output results**: Print each sales amount on a separate line

## Example Verification

Given input:
```
3 10  → B seat: 10 tickets → 10 × 3000 = 30,000
1 4   → S seat: 4 tickets → 4 × 6000 = 24,000
4 1   → C seat: 1 ticket → 1 × 2000 = 2,000
2 5   → A seat: 5 tickets → 5 × 4000 = 20,000
```

```
30000
24000
2000
20000
```

```python
    quantity = int(line[1])
    
    # Calculate sales amount
    sales_amount = price_map[ticket_type] * quantity
    
    # Output the result
    print(sales_amount)
```
